Exact-count rule: if a figure is approximate, write "~" and keep it out of KPI claims. KPI claims require the recount method, sample size, and date.

Main lessons so far:
- 2026-06-13 COMPREHENSIVE SPORT DATA MODEL AND DEEP FEATURE RULE (combined-doc Sections 19-24, attaches to Gates 3-7 and Gate 9): every serious card now inherits the universal data chain of verified source -> timestamped raw data -> role/context adjustment -> matchup model -> probability output -> confidence score -> post-match review. Add written lines for SOURCE CONFIDENCE / DISAGREEMENT, SIMILAR-CONDITION FORM, ROLE / REPLACEMENT / BENCH, STYLE FIT, VOLATILITY / STAT STABILITY, MOTIVATION / NEWS / MARKET CONTEXT, and PREDICTION TRIGGERS. Missing relevant fields are UNKNOWN and cap confidence when they matter.
- 2026-06-13 STATMUSE PRIORITY STRENGTHENING: for every American sports league or U.S./North American league StatMuse covers, StatMuse is the primary statistical access point for L5/L10/L15/L20, season averages, historical/career baselines, phase splits, H2H, venue/home-away splits, and player windows. Official league/team sources still control volatile facts and settlement. Validate StatMuse answer wording, timeframe, table dates, and playoff/regular-season scope before use.
- 2026-06-12 FORM DELTA RULE (combined-doc Section 13, attaches to Gates 6-7): for every decision-driving stat, obtain L5/L10/L15/L20 AND the season average AND a historical baseline (hierarchy: same venue/fixture last 3 seasons -> prior seasons under same coach/core -> multi-season/league baseline), write the FORM DELTA TABLE with sport-scaled divergence thresholds and a verdict (SURGING/IMPROVING/STABLE/DECLINING/COLLAPSING/VOLATILE), and resolve L5-vs-L20 conflicts in writing via opponent-quality adjustment, in-window regime change, or the Gate 6 default (FORM ANCHOR line). The old "(if available, move on)" opt-out is retired for decision-driving stats: missing windows are listed as MISSING and a missing L5/L10 caps confidence. A 5-game delta never anchors #1 without a named mechanism. |Season - Historical| beyond threshold = REGIME FLAG with a one-sentence cause.
- 2026-06-12 PRESS-SIGNAL RULE (combined-doc Section 14, attaches to Gates 4/5/9): every serious card carries a PRESS SIGNAL line; quotes are verbatim with speaker, role, date, and source, classified S1 availability/workload, S2 tempo/aggression intent, S3 identity contradiction (e.g. a defensive-identity coach publicly demanding more shots/offense), S4 accountability callout, S5 conditions read, S6 smokescreen risk. S3 is explicitly UNVALIDATED â€” no trustworthy public base rate exists â€” so until the ledger holds >=20 graded S3 samples it may only colour the writeup or break adjacent-rank ties; it can NEVER flip #1/#4 or override counted frequencies. Every logged signal records a direction hypothesis at card time and an outcome at settlement; classes earn rank-moving weight only at >=20 samples and >=60% directional accuracy, and are inverted/retired below 45% over >=20.
- 2026-06-12 OFFICIALS RULE (combined-doc Section 15 + Source Registry officials table): every serious card carries an OFFICIALS line (names + source + timestamp, or NOT YET ASSIGNED with the known release timing, or SOURCE UNAVAILABLE after walking the chain). Release timings verified 2026-06-12: NBA ~9:00am ET game day at official.nba.com Today's Officials; MLB crew rotates HP->3B->2B->1B within a series and is confirmed day-of via Gameday/Stats API officials block; AFL via the AFLUA per-round appointments PDF midweek; NRL with the Tuesday team lists via Zero Tackle/League Unlimited; EPL midweek "Match officials for Matchweek N" on premierleague.com; cricket via ICC series appointments and the match-page officials tab. Officials are environment modifiers for totals/props only â€” never a side anchor, and never more than one adjacent-rank move without an opened counted tendency table.
- 2026-06-12 RULESET-LOCK GOVERNANCE UPGRADE: every new card must declare competition, governing body, rulebook/playing-conditions edition, competition variant, and settlement convention before ranking. If any ruleset field is UNKNOWN, cap confidence and state the dependency. Retrospectives must classify serious misses as wrong ruleset, wrong availability/eligibility, wrong settlement convention, source-status error, projection error, or rank-slot calibration error.
- 2026-06-10 GOVERNING UPGRADE: before any new ranked card, run the combined-doc "2026-06-10 COMPREHENSIVE ACCURACY UPGRADE" Gate Sequence (Gates 0-10) and show the outputs in the card: GAME-STATE line (request AEST vs venue-local start converted to AEST -> PREGAME/LIVE/LIKELY COMPLETE), source+timestamp+access tags on decisive facts (OPENED/SNIPPET/ASSUMED - no #1 on ASSUMED facts), written projection per market BEFORE ranking, written probability table per option, top-slot stress test (>=60% + fresh mechanism, kill-path named), bottom-slot test (<=30% + most failure paths, swap test, forbidden-#4 list), and chat ranking identical to logged ranking. KPI targets: top hit rate >=80%, bottom-pick win rate <=30% rolling 20 cards.
- 2026-06-10 SECOND-PASS BLINDSPOT FIXES (combined-doc Sections 7-8): (1) EXTREME-SLOT PAIR RULE - never put the two sides of the SAME line at #1 and #4 unless structural-class (<~15% wrong); one directional miss there costs BOTH penalties (it double-penalized Bangladesh-Australia, Antwerp-Akcel, Liege-Akcel, and JB Bruges in one week) - give the extremes to the pair with the strongest directional confidence, coinflip pair to #2/#3. (2) Probabilities must be FREQUENCY-COUNTED from the recency stack (e.g. "hit 6 of last 10"), not narrated. (3) Gradeability pre-check - legs with no settlement source (friendly corners, minor-league phases) stay out of extreme slots. (4) State settlement conventions (OT included / 90' only / pushes / DLS). (5) Soccer winner leans state home/draw/away three ways - the draw can be the modal outcome. (6) A line >1.5 units from projection is a format/news ALARM before it is an edge. (7) Log first, copy ranking to chat verbatim. (8) StatMuse "this season" may exclude playoffs - query playoff windows explicitly in postseason. (9) Pre-flag void risk and prefer survivable legs at extremes. (10) Walk the access fallback chain (alternate domain -> API -> r.jina.ai proxy -> dated snippets) before writing UNKNOWN; access status as of 2026-06-10 is in doc Section 8 (NRL.com login-walled -> zerotackle; ESPNcricinfo via proxy; NHL via ESPN API; Cricbuzz/NDTV fluctuate - retry each session).
- 2026-06-10 THIRD-PASS (late-day) - combined-doc Sections 10-11 added. (a) SOCIAL ACCESS RETEST: X is hard-blocked logged-out (HTTP 402 on x.com AND publish.x.com/oembed; syndication endpoint empty/429); r.jina.ai reads X post text but WITHOUT timestamps (SNIPPET-grade only); Reddit is blocked on EVERY automated route (www/old/api .json, proxy, search index, shell) - write "SOCIAL CHECK: reddit unavailable" instead of claiming radar coverage. NEW WORKING RUNG: Bluesky public API (public.api.bsky.app - searchActors/getProfile/getAuthorFeed, no key, ISO timestamps; official nba.com domain-verified handle had posts within ~2h; tweet-mirror bots = Tier C discovery only). (b) FETCH TRUNCATION TRAP: summarized fetches cut big JSONs (the 2026-06-10 ESPN MLB slate rendered as 6 of 15 games) - slate counts only via direct shell fetch. (c) RANK-SLOT META-CALIBRATION (parse of all 172 active rows): RAW slot hit rates #1 64.8%, #2 57.1%, #3 47.9%, #4 49.4% - the published ~82.7% top rate is the penalty ledger AFTER exemptions, not the raw rate; the ranking currently separates halves, not slots. Per-sport: Euro/domestic basketball is the only sport where #4 is a true fade (28% bottom-win); KBO is near-INVERTED (#4 won 6 of 7, #1 3W-4L); cricket T20 has a systematic UNDER-BIAS (13/17 buried overs won, 9/12 lost #1 anchors were unders); MLB #4 wins 58.3% despite the best #1 (76.2%); NBA Q1/1H pair cards show no edge at either extreme. New gates now in doc Section 11: T20 PP/innings overs cannot sit at #4 without structural evidence; KBO #4 structural-only with card-wide confidence cap; NBA phase pairs are coinflip-class; leak-flagged sports (MLB/Cricket/AFL/KBO/NBA-phase) require a written SPORT-LEAK CHECK line in every card. (d) Section 12 added: reproducible procedures - access-probe protocol (search-index blocks are separate from fetch blocks; test both), direct shell fetch patterns for slate counts (anti-truncation), the log-recount methodology with its parsing pitfalls (case-mixed verdicts, parenthetical false tokens - strip parens first), and evidence standards for doc updates (test dates on access claims, method+sample size on stats, every rule names its failure/gate/required written output).
- Research-only mode is working, but it needs disciplined post-query grading.
- Cross-sport lineup evidence rule: before every ranked card, build a timestamped availability packet for both teams using official injury reports, final lineups/team sheets/inactives/toss XI/game centers, plus official team PR or credentialed reporting where needed. Keep availability, starting status, bench role, minutes/workload limits, and actual participation as separate facts. Do not let rank 1 or rank 4 depend on an unresolved Tier A/Tier B player status.
- DATA CUTOFF rule update: the cutoff is the exact time the user sends the request, in the user's active timezone unless otherwise stated. Pre-game analysis may only use information available at or before that request time; later facts are for live corrections, settlements, and retrospectives only.
- Timezone/date rule for prediction requests: this workspace/request context is Australia/Sydney, and June is AEST (UTC+10). Treat user-facing request dates/times as AEST unless the user explicitly states otherwise. Always preserve the event source's local timezone and convert the official start time to AEST before deciding whether the game is today's, tomorrow's, already live, complete, or tied to a previous-day local fixture; a game in another timezone may have an official local date one day earlier than the AEST request date.
- Pending-log sweep rule: at the start of every new user request, check pending/live/incomplete log rows. Clear any finished game, quarter, half, innings, period, or market component using reliable current sources; if data is still missing or the event is not finished, keep it pending and carry it forward to the next request.
- Source-audit correction rule: when a sweep finds a stale or incorrect final score, correct the row even if it changes the winner grade, and do not infer unresolved phase markets from a rough score pace. Use a final/complete score source for the match result and a separate over-by-over/period source for phase legs.
- Void/canceled fixture rule: if a match is canceled, abandoned before settlement, or never reaches the required market condition, mark the card VOID/UNGRADED and remove it from pending. Do not force wins/losses from a listed 0-0 placeholder or a pre-kickoff canceled score.
- AFL low-total side-axis correction: when the expected total is low and the home underdog has an elite defense, the underdog cushion becomes more valuable, not less. Missing forwards can strengthen the total under without justifying a large favorite cover; scoring-shot conversion variance can decide the side in a one-score game.
- KBO starter-and-live-state correction: do not let season H2H, a hitter-friendly park, or prior-series scoring bury a broad high under when a credible starter is in current form. If the underdog leads by the middle innings with a large hit advantage, discard the pregame favorite/over narrative and rerank from the live contact and bullpen state.
- ODI phase-market settlement correction: settle completed phase legs independently from a still-live innings. A later published score can prove an earlier threshold without an exact milestone figure when scoring is monotonic; Australia being only 23/1 after six overs conclusively settled its first-five Under 26.5 as a win and Over 26.5 as a loss.
- New grading rule: the fourth-ranked pick is a declared least-likely outcome. If it wins, count the normal item result and add one extra bottom-pick penalty loss because the ranking missed the bottom outcome completely, EXCEPT under the top-half sweep exemption below.
- Top-half sweep exemption (2026-06-12, user directive): if every pick in the top half of the ranked card wins, do NOT apply an extra bottom-pick penalty even if the bottom pick also wins. For 4-option cards, "top half" means ranks 1-2; for 8-option cards, it means ranks 1-4. Still record the bottom pick's normal win/loss result, but no added bottom-pick penalty or mandatory serious bottom-slot retrospective is needed because the strongest cluster was correctly selected.
- Top-pick grading rule: the first-ranked pick is the declared strongest edge. If it loses, count the normal item result and add one extra top-pick penalty loss, then write a serious retrospective explaining what was overweighted, what warning signs were missed, and what should change in future ranking logic.
- Top-two failure review rule (2026-06-10, user directive): ranks 1 and 2 are the declared strongest cluster. If either one fails, run serious introspection with comprehensive source research before the next comparable card: verify final/phase result, re-check availability, lineups, weather/venue, tactical/press-conference context, form, matchup data, and source freshness; then add any concrete learning to the sports doc. If both top-two picks fail, treat it as a major card-level process failure even if the raw W-L record is mixed. This rule adds mandatory review and learning, while the separate top-pick penalty still applies only when rank 1 loses unless another grading rule says otherwise.
- Confidence-weighted grading rule (2026-06-04, user directive): weight results by rank. If either and/or both BOTTOM TWO picks (ranks 3 and 4) LOSE, that is the expected result and counts as only HALF weight (half win / at most half loss) - it confirms the ranking and does NOT trigger serious introspection. If the BOTTOM pick (rank 4) WINS and/or the TOP pick (rank 1) LOSES, that is a full-weight failure at the most confident point: apply the existing top/bottom penalty AND write a serious retrospective with one concrete rule change. This sits on top of the existing W-L and penalty bookkeeping; see combined-doc "CARD GRADING WEIGHT RULE".
- One-goal margin grading rule (2026-06-09, user directive): if a TOTAL GOALS market wins or loses by a single goal (a one-goal near-miss either side of the line, e.g. line 2.5 landing on exactly 2 or exactly 3), do NOT apply a double/penalty weighting to it - grade it as a normal single win or loss. The ONLY exception: if that total-goals pick was the declared BOTTOM pick AND it lost, the standard bottom-pick penalty still applies. In all other slots a one-goal swing is treated as an ordinary result, not a confidence failure.
- Coach/player press-conference rule: include relevant pre-match and post-match press conference/report material when it can affect or explain the card. Pregame use: availability, workload, rotation, tactical intent, pitch/venue read, weather prep, motivation, and role allocation. Postgame use: explain misses and add lessons only when comments are official/credentialed and supported by the box score, phase splits, or live evidence. If no reliable press source opens, write PRESS COMMENTS: not found/opened rather than inventing intent.
- T20 powerplay calls need heavier weighting on pitch report, boundary dimensions, and actual powerplay bowling form than on broad season batting form.
- At Wankhede or similar flat, fast-scoring venues, do not rank a first-innings under around 200-205 as the top pick when the pitch report is strongly batting-friendly and the batting XI contains multiple power hitters.
- At Arun Jaitley/Delhi-type short-boundary venues, do not let "possible early movement" override short boundaries, true bounce, clear weather, and an explosive opener profile. If the pitch report says high-scoring is possible if batters capitalize, powerplay overs near 60 must stay above broad high unders unless there is concrete swing/seam evidence.
- At Chepauk/MA Chidambaram, split the powerplay and innings models. The hard new ball can make modest powerplay overs live, but dry third-use surfaces and middle-over spin/change-up grip can still keep 207+ innings totals upper-tail.
- In basketball playoff finals/rematches on a one-day turnaround, the prior same-series quarter and half splits are the closest equivalent to live conditions. If Game 1 at the same venue clears Q1 and 1H thresholds comfortably and no major rotation change appears, do not downgrade the Game 2 over merely because "finals get tighter."
- But do not treat one high-scoring Game 1 as automatically repeatable in a finals Game 2. Check whether the opener was driven by hot shooting, transition leakage, fouls/free throws, or one team failing a specific coverage. A 24-hour rematch can compress pace and scoring through defensive corrections and fatigue even with the same venue and rosters.
- Basketball domestic-final Game 2 rule: after a Game 1 blowout, separate winner, spread, Q1, 1H, and full total. Immediate Game 1 phase splits and official/credentialed comments about defensive physicality or a blocked early offense should control phase modelling, but a venue switch and large road-favorite handicap can still make the underdog cushion live even when the favorite remains the clear winner.
- In basketball cards with high exact-complement thresholds, same-season H2H phase splits should control the ranking when they repeatedly sit under the threshold. Recent full-game shootouts can raise over risk, but they should not push Q1/1H overs above unders unless the direct matchup or confirmed lineup changes support faster early scoring.
- In WNBA early-season exact-complement cards, do not use full-game totals alone. Compare Q1 and 1H phase splits separately; first-half overs can outrank Q1 overs when the expected path is a normal/fast Q1 with second-quarter recovery from bench scoring, foul pressure, or defensive slippage.
- In NBA playoff Game 1 cards with asymmetric Q1/1H thresholds, separate regular-season H2H pace from current injury state. If a primary ball-handler is ruled out late, keep broad high unders above overs unless the lower over remains clearly inside the projected phase band.
- In playoff semifinal rematches, use the immediate Game 1 Q1 and 1H split even when the final margin was large. A 90-point favorite performance can still hide a 30-point Q1 and 72-point half, meaning the rematch can favor early unders unless the underdog's home adjustment clearly raises pace.
- Live wickets matter, but they do not automatically kill a 220+ innings if the side is already 150+ before the 12th over on a flat pitch.
- Wide "under high threshold" totals have generally been more reliable than narrow overs when collapse or middle-over spin/bowling pressure exists.
- MLB opener/bullpen-game classification matters. A listed starter who is actually an opener/reliever must not be modelled as a normal starter.
- Elite starting pitching does not automatically create a full-game under in homer-friendly parks against power lineups in strong recent form. Model the ace-damage home-run path separately.
- In MLB low-total roof/park-suppressed games, a +1.5 cushion can outrank a no-cushion favorite even when the favorite has the better starter. If the expected cluster is 5-7 runs, one-run and underdog-cover paths are live unless the favorite has a clear lineup and bullpen edge.
- If the user challenges a prediction or live state is available, refresh the score/state and correct quickly.
- If the named fixture and the listed market teams do not match, flag it and anchor the analysis to the verifiable listed items rather than silently blending fixtures.
- If the innings/team market names a side not playing in the listed cricket match, treat the card as invalid until clarified; do not silently convert the team name or log a graded prediction under the wrong batting side.
- After a correct explosive-phase call, avoid overfitting the next cricket match. Re-check whether the new batting side has the same recent phase pattern and whether the new venue, boundary size, moisture, or missing finisher caps the ceiling.
- Basketball quarter and half totals must be separated: a game can clear Q1 over and still stay under the first-half number if the second quarter cools, especially in European lower-tempo leagues with bench/half-court stretches.
- In domestic top-v-bottom mismatches, the favorite can create a strong first-half over window before late rotation or garbage-time cooling; recent H2H quarter/half splits matter more than full-game average alone.
- WNBA early-season totals: opener quarter/half splits, roster continuity, missing defenders, and short benches can matter more than full-season priors. A first-half over can be stronger than Q1 over when both teams' openers ran hot but Q1 has more rotation/nerves volatility.
- WNBA exact-complement cards near 43.5 Q1 and 86.5 1H need threshold-distance discipline. If both teams' recent phase averages sit right around the line, returning offensive starters and defensive/foul concerns can lean over, but do not describe the under as dead; cap confidence and rank by the cleaner phase edge.
- WNBA short-window rematch correction: do not assume two prior same-season Q1/1H overs repeat when the most recent first half barely cleared and one offense remains fragile. Defensive adjustments, tired legs, and Q2 shot-creation collapse can flip both early totals; keep the exact under complements closer unless current lineup/creation evidence is stronger.
- Fixture/team-label mismatch rule confirmed: if the listed innings market names a team not playing in the fixture, mark the written market invalid/mismatched and only provide a conditional interpretation if the intended batting team is obvious from toss and match context.
- Ekana/Lucknow live correction: do not over-cap the powerplay from missing openers when the pitch report also gives tiny square boundaries and the replacement top order has boundary hitters. Early wickets can coexist with a 60+ powerplay if one set batter and one attacking partner exploit pace-on bowling.
- Estonian KML finals exact-complement cards need immediate series phase weighting and low confidence when Games 1-2 split around the threshold. A 40/49 Q1 and 77/82 first-half sequence points to a narrow 41-44 Q1 and 79-83 half band, not a confident all-over or all-under package.
- NZNBL main-game totals must stay separated from Rapid League fixtures and scores. When the official schedule lists Rapid/NBL as consecutive start times, use only the main NBL score and main-game phase profile for user Q1/1H totals.
- NZNBL live correction: when the main-game Q1 reaches the mid-50s, immediately flip first-half modelling. A 56-point Q1 leaves only 40 points needed for Over 95.5, so the first-half over becomes more likely than the under unless there is clear foul trouble, injury, or bench-unit stall evidence.
- Basketball high-Q1 regression rule: after a very high-scoring first quarter, do not automatically project the same pace into Q2. Recent cards show Q2 can reduce sharply through bench units, defensive adjustment, fatigue, shot-quality regression, and possession control; rerank first-half totals from the exact Q2 points needed and the live rotation/foul context.
- NZNBL high-Q1 settlement correction: Taranaki-Canterbury confirmed both truths at once. Q1 reached 56, Q2 cooled to 46, but the first-half still reached 102 because only 40 Q2 points were needed. Use exact remaining points, not a blanket "Q2 regression means half under" rule.
- Basketball phase-data requirement: for any Q1, Q2, or first-half prediction/settlement, collect and log the actual quarter scores and halftime total whenever available. Full-time score alone is not enough because it can hide Q1 pace, Q2 regression, overtime, or late blowout scoring.
- PBA import-replacement rule: when a high-usage import is ruled out and a replacement import is rushed in on short notice, downgrade sustained first-half offensive continuity even if Q1 energy remains live. Separate early emotional pace from Q2 integration/bench-settling risk.
- PBA side/total split: when an immediate semifinal series has totals clustered around the user threshold but the same tactical adjustment repeatedly creates a side edge, rank the spread/side above the total. Ginebra-Rain or Shine Game 1 landed 226, Game 2 landed 210, and May 3 landed 204, making 210.5 a thinner call than the Ginebra adjustment/winner read.
- Wankhede high-threshold cricket rule: on a batting-friendly Wankhede surface with short boundaries, dew/chasing preference, and an explosive top order batting first, keep 211+ innings overs above the direct under unless there is elite bowling/early movement evidence. But powerplay overs near 67 remain a separate high bar and can rank below the innings over.
- Best-of-three basketball playoff series: immediate Games 1-2 quarter/half splits should be weighted heavily. If both first halves clear but Q1 is split, the first-half over is more robust than the Q1 over.
- Add phase/period splits to future analysis and retrospectives: cricket powerplay/middle/death overs, MLB inning-by-inning and bullpen-entry points, basketball quarter/half splits, soccer half splits, AFL quarter splits. Final scores can hide which team controlled most of the game.
- For exact paired thresholds, identify the direct complement relationship and rank the expected band clearly rather than pretending both sides are independent.
- Overtime and late-period surges must be modelled explicitly for basketball totals/spreads; full-game unders can be good in regulation and still lose if OT is included.
- Live pitch/captain comments in cricket must be weighted above venue averages when they are supported by immediate powerplay scoring.
- In MLB, weak starter contact profiles plus vulnerable bullpens can make modest overs stronger than high-threshold unders even in cool/neutral weather.
- In MLB, avoid making a strong home team +1.5 the bottom pick when the main game script is low or middle scoring; starter edge alone does not erase close-game/home-bullpen paths.
- In AFL, late scratches of elite ball users must immediately downgrade that team's cover/scoring projection, especially in hot or humid neutral/alternate venues where clean transition is already harder.
- Added source material: halftime_under_strategy_report.pdf. Key rule: separate full-game under logic from first-half/first-5 under logic; full-game unders must survive overtime, garbage time/fouling, bullpen exposure, and late pace changes, while early-phase unders are cleaner when the risk is mainly late.
- Cricket pitch reports now carry stronger priority: if a live/official report says hard/even batting surface, 200+ venue first-innings average, short boundary, breeze/carry, and plenty of runs expected, modest powerplay/innings overs cannot be ranked last without a clear opposing bowling/weather reason.
- Cricket phase split correction: a massive 250+ first innings can still have a merely strong, not extreme, powerplay. Do not put a broad high powerplay under last when the expected PP band includes the low/mid-60s; middle-overs/death acceleration can deliver the innings over even if PP Over fails or only narrowly wins.
- Positive cricket confirmation: when a side has repeated recent powerplay explosions and the pitch report supports batting, trust the phase evidence early. LSG's 91-run powerplay vs CSK reinforced the revised over-first approach.
- Jaipur/Sawai Mansingh live pitch rule: if the broadcast report says flat as a pancake, rock hard, no live grass, 65m square boundaries, and the only prior game on that strip had 228 chased down, treat 196+ innings and 53+ powerplay overs as primary paths. Do not let older Jaipur averages or generic chasing preference suppress the batting-first over read.
- T20 knockout/powerplay correction: when a batting-friendly pitch and an in-form explosive opener put 67-70 powerplay runs inside the live projection, do not make a high powerplay over the bottom pick just because early wickets or strong new-ball names exist. The innings can still land inside a correct 205-224 middle band while the powerplay over wins.
- Basketball consolation/third-place/dead-rubber context can override normal playoff-under logic: lower consequence can increase shot freedom and early pace, especially when both teams' offensive averages already sit above the user threshold.
- MLB no-cushion favorite vs broad run cushion: when handedness splits and starter volatility undercut the favorite, a +2.5 can be more likely than the favorite to win even if the favorite has the better roster/name starter.
- MLB correction: do not overcorrect by putting the favorite to win last when home/team-quality edge still creates many ordinary win paths. A +2.5 cushion can be safer than the moneyline, but the moneyline is not automatically the least likely.
- In roof-closed, pitcher-friendly MLB parks, wide high-threshold unders and both +1.5 cushions can outrank a modest over when the likely score band is 6-8 rather than 8-10.
- Cricket same-matchup outliers must be venue-adjusted: do not blindly carry a 500+ aggregate or 70+ powerplay from a flat chase into a fresh-wicket first innings with early seam/carry, cooler weather, changed bowling personnel, or different innings pressure.
- Cricket close-miss refinement: at altitude/short-boundary venues, a fresh wicket and new-ball seam can still coexist with a 70+ powerplay if wides/extras and one opener's boundary burst arrive. Treat high powerplay unders near 70 as a lean, not an anchor, when venue carry and batting form both support scoring.
- Cricket batting-first ceiling check: when a side like GT has a stability-first top order and a fragile/less explosive middle order, a batting-friendly surface can still point to the 187-202 middle band rather than a 203+ innings if the opposition chose to bowl for dew and has early hard-length pace.
- Basketball paired Q1/1H totals can point in different directions when the profile is low-to-mid Q1 then Q2 scoring recovery. Rank the expected band explicitly instead of packaging all overs or all unders together.
- NBA playoff same-series rematches need phase separation: recent same-venue pace matters, but Q1 overs and 1H overs can split if the second quarter cools through bench units, half-court possessions, or defensive adjustments.
- NBA exact-complement Game 2 cards need the immediate Game 1 Q1/1H split as the primary signal, with a repeatability test. If Game 1 started slow because of cold shooting and turnovers but Q2 recovered, keep the unders first at high thresholds, but rank the first-half over above the Q1 over because Q2 gives recovery time.
- PBA playoff rematches need immediate H2H weighting: when the same teams played three days earlier and produced a very low Q1/1H, that direct phase evidence can override recent high-scoring games against weaker or import-limited opponents.
- Domestic top-seed playoff mismatches can favor first-half over without requiring Q1 over. A favorite's defensive start can suppress Q1, while Q2 depth and mismatch pressure still push the half above the threshold.
- WNBA expansion-team unders need special attention early: if the opener shows low assists, poor shooting, high turnovers, and free-throw-dependent scoring, do not let opponent pace alone override the offensive-cohesion drag.
- When revised basketball thresholds overlap widely, rerank from the new middle band instead of preserving the old lean. Broad middle-band covers should rise above thinner edges even when the game script is unchanged.
- WNBA injury clusters need two-way modelling: missing frontcourt players can suppress that team's scoring while making the opponent's modest over more likely through weakened rim protection and rebounding.
- In WNBA overlapping threshold cards, do not treat a low Q1 over as automatically safe. If multiple recent Q1s landed exactly 37 and the opponent defense is strong, the wider 1H middle-band cover can be safer.
- For NZNBL and smaller leagues with limited starter transparency, mark starters as PROJECTED unless confirmed. Recent H2H first-half suppression should outweigh broad recent full-game overs when the projected cores and matchup are similar.
- For NZNBL and similar leagues with Rapid League curtain-raisers, verify competition label and tip time before using live scores; do not import Rapid League quarter/half data into the main NBL market.
- NZNBL high exact-complement Q1/1H cards need home/away phase separation. Season PPG can suggest a 190+ full-game environment, but if the home team's actual home starts are 30-51 Q1 and 73-93 1H, high early unders should outrank overs unless current lineup/pace evidence says the home trend has changed.
- NPB baseball needs its own source stack: use NPB.jp and Yahoo Japan Sports for official schedule, starters, confirmed "starting" lineups, team stats, and weather. In Central League games, explicitly model the pitcher batting spot and the lower NPB scoring environment before overreacting to weak starter ERAs.

- NPB draw correction: in low-scoring projected games, both +1.5 cushions can survive a 12-inning draw. Do not put either +1.5 side last unless the favorite's multi-run separation path is clearly stronger than the draw/one-run path.
- NPB Pacific League overlapping totals: when the card pairs a no-cushion favorite ML with an opponent +1.5 and Over 7.5/Under 9.5, prioritize the likely 6-9 run cluster. A broad high under and the underdog cushion can outrank the favorite ML when the starter matchup and NPB draw/close-game rules support one-run or low-margin outcomes.
- NPB Pacific League exact 6.5 totals: do not default to under only because of the league run environment. A 6.5 threshold can be cleared by a normal 4-3/5-2 script when confirmed lineups have platoon paths, H2H totals sit around 7+, or bullpen/fielding risk adds one run.
- NPB starter-edge sides need a fragility check. If the preferred side is anchored mainly to the starter, an emergency first-inning exit can flip the side; if the opposing starter has recent 7-inning stability, that opponent ML should not be buried even when winless.
- T20 paired-threshold correction: when a middle band can cash both sides, rank the bottom pick as the outcome with the most failure paths, not merely the side that failed last match. At altitude venues, modest powerplay overs stay live, but a broad high powerplay under can still be safer if the pitch report flags early dual pace and bowler assistance.
- Eden Gardens T20 paired thresholds: if the current pitch/venue report is batting-friendly and recent Eden innings are 220+, keep modest innings and powerplay overs live. But when MI/another power lineup bats first before dew against a dry or covered strip with Narine/Varun-type spin available, the likely powerplay can sit in the 55-64 middle band while the innings still pushes 190-214. Do not package both overs or both unders together.
- Ahmedabad/Narendra Modi red-soil T20 rule: when the pitch report says red soil, hard surface, grass rolled in, pace/carry, little spin, and one short square boundary, keep modest GT/strong-top-order overs live. But because the same setup gives quicks early pace and bounce, separate a 55-64 powerplay band from a 189-208 innings band rather than forcing an all-over script.
- MLB overlapping totals need exact middle-band logic: Over 7.0 and Under 9.0 both win at 8 runs. Do not force them into opposite sides; rank them by the most likely run cluster and how weather, starter quality, and bullpen fatigue shape 7/8/9-run outcomes.
- MLB whole-number high unders need push-aware middle-band logic. If the likely score cluster is 9-10, Under 11 can still rank first while Over 8.5 also stays live; do not bury the modest over merely because the high under is strongest. Use the fourth slot for the side/total with the most failure paths outside the projected band.
- MLB same-series offensive avalanche correction: do not treat a high under as strongest when the previous game in the same series already produced 20 runs and both starters/bullpens have WHIP/command or depth issues. Even at a neutral park/weather setup, early traffic plus one defensive mistake or one homer can turn an 8-10 band into 12+ before the bullpens stabilize.
- MLB exact 7.5 totals in cool or park-suppressed games need 7-8 cluster handling. Do not call the under safe when weak defense, walk traffic, bullpen volatility, or same-series scoring can produce a single extra run; keep Over 7.5 and Under 7.5 adjacent unless one side has clear separation.
- KBO exact 8.5 correction: if the realistic band includes 8 runs, do not put Under 8.5 last just because current form points up. One team can win 5-3 through a single crooked inning, HBP/walk pressure, and fielding mistakes while the opponent's offense stays mostly quiet.
- AFL overlapping-total lesson, corrected: late key-forward outs can reduce a favourite's scoring ceiling, but weather must be tied to the actual match window. Do not use whole-day showers as a scoring downgrade if rain is forecast mainly after the game.
- AFL live correction lesson: if a favourite is already 80+ points at halftime or leads by 50+, pregame weather/key-forward-out logic is invalidated. Treat territory dominance and opponent defensive collapse as the new primary evidence, and move the low over and favourite line sharply upward.
- AFL weather process correction: before any outdoor AFL pick, record hourly venue weather for one hour before bounce, each quarter window, and one hour after. Only apply rain/slippery-ball downgrades for rain expected before or during the match, or for a surface already made wet.
- WEATHER GAME-WINDOW RULE (2026-06-08, user directive - applies to ALL outdoor games, every sport): research weather specifically inside the 30-60 minute window BEFORE, DURING, and AFTER the scheduled start, in the VENUE'S LOCAL time (convert from the request timezone first - they can differ). A whole-day gameday preview is fine for context, but the decision-driving read must be the actual in-window conditions, using live/nowcast data when the game is near/in progress. Only apply a rain/wet/slippery scoring downgrade if rain is expected before or during the match window or the surface is already wet - a morning-rain forecast that clears before start is NOT a wet-match downgrade. Trigger: 2026-06-08 AFL Collingwood-Melbourne, where I wrongly called it rain-affected pregame; user flagged it was dry and WillyWeather confirmed 0mm through the 3:15-6pm window. See memory/weather-game-window-rule.md.
- AFL exact-complement total/spread cards: do not let a favorite win streak automatically drive a big handicap cover. Compare the favorite's recent margins against strong opponents separately from the total, and let the total lead when both teams' recent scoring profiles sit above the user threshold.
- WNBA expansion-team correction: do not let a terrible debut shooting game suppress every early-period over if the next game immediately shows Q1 regression. Seattle-Toronto reached 49 in Q1 and 89 by halftime after Toronto's 27% FG debut; broad high unders can still be safest, but a low Q1 over should not be buried when the expected middle band includes it.
- Cricket XI-vs-pitch refinement: at Dharamshala/HPCA, current-season 200+ venue data and batting-friendly pitch reports should lift innings overs, but confirmed absences of powerplay hitters or key middle-order accelerators can cap the upper powerplay/innings ceiling. Treat that as a middle-band setup, not an automatic 215+/65+ script.
- WNBA expansion-team overlapping bands: separate broad upper-unders from low overs by phase. If the expansion team has volatile Q1 creation and questionable guard depth, the broad Q1 upper-under can outrank the low Q1 over even when the opponent's recent Q1s are fast; first-half middle-band covers can still lead because Q2 recovery exists.
- WNBA exact-complement injury clusters: when an expansion team loses multiple frontcourt players, split offense and defense effects by phase. Missing size can lift the opponent's early paint/free-throw scoring while also making the expansion team's own half-court scoring fragile; this can push 1H over above 1H under even when Q1 remains close to the threshold.
- ABA semifinal exact-complement totals: do not automatically downgrade overs because of playoff stakes if direct H2Hs and immediate playoff games repeatedly opened at 49-50 Q1 and 87-100 by halftime. Neutral-site relocation can reduce home-court edge, but it does not erase two elite offenses unless injuries materially remove creation.
- Israeli National League exact-complement totals: when direct league H2Hs repeatedly show low Q1 and first-half totals but full games open up later, rank first-half/Q1 unders above overs even if season defensive averages are poor. Treat cup shootouts and late-season dead-rubber looseness as over risk, not the primary signal.
- BBL playoff exact-complement totals: do not let a high final score override the same-series early phase split. If Game 1 had a 46-point Q1 but only 87 by halftime, and the favorite is expected to respond with more physical defense, Q1/1H unders can outrank overs even when full-game scoring and season PPG are high.
- KBO venue verification matters: Samsung home games can move from Daegu to Pohang, so do not import Daegu's homer/run environment automatically. Use KBO/MyKBO official fixture venue, confirmed lineups, starters, team splits, and hourly stadium weather before ranking totals.
- KBO correction: do not treat a top-table game with two credible starters as automatically under-friendly when both teams have strong season run production and the venue/weather can carry. A 12-run outcome can still arrive through middle/late contact even if the early starter script looks controlled.
- Soccer first-half overlapping goals: Over 0.5 and Under 2.5 both win at exactly 1 or 2 first-half goals. Rank the broad upper-under first when a three-goal first half needs a defensive collapse, but do not bury the low over if recent goal timing, team motivation, or opponent defensive weakness points to an early goal.
- Soccer narrow first-half overlap: Over 0.5 and Under 1.5 both win only at exactly 1 first-half goal. Compare 0-0 risk directly against 2+ first-half-goal risk; recent H2H halftime scores and confirmed attacking absences should decide which failure path is thinner.
- Soccer live correction: do not overweight old exact-one-goal H2H halves when the current confirmed XIs include multiple pace/transition forwards and both current defenses are conceding heavily. In that setup, 2+ first-half goals can be more likely than the stale H2H sample implies, so a low first-half over should not be buried.
- PBA import paperwork/clearance rule: do not treat a replacement import as available until official or highly reliable reporting confirms clearance. If the import is only expected but not processed, model both Q1 and 1H as all-Filipino/continuity-risk states and downgrade early overs as well as sustained-half overs.
- Late-season basketball motivation rule: a mathematically safe home team can play with less resistance than the standings/table suggest when the opponent is fighting relegation or elimination. In ACB and similar leagues, road record should not override current survival urgency, confirmed motivation, and current shooting role/availability.
- Basketball title-clinch/rematch side rule: early phase totals can be correct while the side is wrong. If a team leads a series 2-0 but the opponent is a defending champion or deeper late-game team, price fourth-quarter response capacity separately from Q1/1H scoring.
- T20 Wankhede high-total correction: do not bury the direct innings under above 211 when the live powerplay is 54/3 or similar and wickets-in-hand are badly damaged. A batting-friendly venue can still land 200-211 through lower-order hitting without clearing a 212+ innings threshold.
- PBA full-game total lesson: Ginebra-Rain or Shine Game 3 showed a fast 104-point first half can still finish under 210.5 if the favourite creates one dominant defensive quarter. Full-game totals in PBA playoff rematches need quarter-specific adjustment paths, not only first-half pace extrapolation.
- American sports source rule: for NBA, WNBA, MLB, NFL, NHL, NCAA/college, and other US/American franchise-sport queries where supported, use StatMuse as a required first statistical lookup. For availability, starting lineups, bench/rotation, injuries, scratches, inactives, starting pitchers/goalies/QBs, and minutes/snap/TOI limits, check official league/team sources and verified team/PR or beat reporting; if not verified, state UNKNOWN and cap confidence.
- American sports StatMuse enforcement reminder (2026-06-12): every American-sports card must include a StatMuse-first statistical check or explicitly state "StatMuse unavailable/misparsed/stale" before using official/stat-site fallbacks. Official league feeds still control volatile facts such as game status, lineups, injuries, probable pitchers, starters, roof/weather, and final settlement.
- MLB 7-8 run cluster confirmation: Twins-White Sox landed 5-3 in 11 innings and Phillies-Padres landed 4-3. Low or middle total bands can make the broad under and modest over both win, while a no-cushion ML can still flip on one swing or one late inning. Do not bury an ordinary ML path merely because the other starter has the cleaner profile.
- NBA Game 5 settlement correction: the final Spurs-Thunder Game 5 first half reached 127 after a 56-point Q1 and a 71-point Q2. The miss was not only home response; it was Q2 foul/free-throw acceleration and Thunder bench/role-player scoring. For playoff 1H markets, model whistle/bonus pressure and bench attack as explicit second-quarter over paths.
- Post-miss overcorrection confirmed: the Game 4 under lesson was real, but the Game 5 response lesson was also real. After a strong correction, do not let the next ranking swing fully to the opposite side without testing home response, lineup replacement quality, foul pressure, and threshold distance.
- T20 live arithmetic override: SRH-RR showed that a pregame 202-221 middle-band read must be discarded when the batting side reaches 80/0 after 6 or 100+ by 7-8 overs on a confirmed flat/short-boundary surface. At that point, required-run math controls: the modest innings over becomes strongest and the broad high under can become weakest unless wickets fall immediately.
- BSL playoff Game 1 total caution: Efes-Turk Telekom finished 86-75 after a pregame over lean. In 4-vs-5 playoff openers, season home averages and one recent 190+ H2H are not enough to bury the under; defensive intent, half-court pace, and opponent scoring control can pull a projected 172-180 band into the low 160s.
- Late-season domestic basketball side-vs-total split: Beer Sheva-Netanya confirmed that the total can be right while the favorite side is wrong. When one team is already safe or qualified and the opponent has relegation/lower-house urgency, keep the motivated underdog cushion live even if H2H and home court favor the favorite.
- INJURY / LAST-MINUTE EXCLUSION RULE (2026-06-04, from Guardians-Yankees and Tempo-Liberty settlements): availability must be re-checked at the latest possible cutoff and weighted by PLAYER PROFILE, not by headcount. A confirmed exclusion of an elite/MVP-tier player (Judge OUT for NY) must collapse any "stronger offense/side/winner" assumption that rested on that player; do not carry a "day-to-day" label into the ranking as if the player is playing. See the dedicated combined-doc module "INJURY AND LAST-MINUTE LINEUP EXCLUSION IMPACT MODEL".
- Star scratch hits the SIDE/winner harder than the TOTAL when the opponent supplies the runs/points: Judge OUT helped sink the Yankees winner lean, but the total still went OVER (9) because Cleveland's power homered. Re-price the winner/side down on a star bat-out, but do not assume the total drops with it if the other team can score.
- Net exclusions by profile across BOTH teams before ranking: in Liberty-Tempo the decisive injury was Toronto's thin frontcourt (Fagbenle/Harrison out) being punished by Jones/Stewart, which outweighed NY missing the perimeter creator Ionescu. A missing interior anchor/elite rebounder on an already-thin unit swings a game more than a missing perimeter creator when the opponent owns the paint.
- Quality-starter-under is a LEAN, not a lock: the Marlins-game "elite starter -> under" rule failed at Yankee Stadium (9 runs) because a homer-friendly park plus an opposing power lineup cleared it even with one offense gutted by injury. Keep the lean, but do not bury the over in a hitter's park against a power lineup.
- SINGLE-QUARTER SPREAD RULE (corrected 2026-06-04, Mercury-Storm Q4): do not treat a cushion as automatically safest or a lay side as automatically weakest. Period margins are volatile and can widen quickly even in low-scoring games. Verify the exact end-of-period score, current run, rotations, foul/bonus state, rest pattern, and which team must chase before ranking. Mercury-Storm correction: Seattle led 55-52 entering Q4, Phoenix won Q4 20-13, Storm +2.5 lost, and Mercury -2.5 won.
- SINGLE-QUARTER TOTAL RULE: anchor a Q4 (or any single-quarter) total to all completed periods and the exact current state, then model late fouling as a conditional branch rather than an automatic over driver. Mercury-Storm Q4 still landed 33 under 39.5 even though the game was close, because Seattle scored only 13 and foul/free-throw inflation did not materialize.
- LIVE PERIOD-STATE INTEGRITY RULE: before grading or ranking a live period, verify the official score at the actual period boundary and reconcile period totals to halftime and final totals. A stale or incorrect live score can invert the side ranking even when the total direction survives.
- LIVE PLAYER POINTS-PROP FRAMEWORK (corrected from Mikal Bridges over 10.5 loss at 9): required-points math is necessary but not sufficient. Estimate remaining shot attempts and free-throw chances from current usage, touches, attempts, role, actual rotation, and teammate takeover risk; do not convert game clock into scoring rate as if full minutes and season usage are guaranteed. A non-blowout does not guarantee starter minutes or attempts, and a cold/low-usage game sample is meaningful when supported by low volume.
- LIVE PLAYER-PROP SETTLEMENT INTEGRITY: verify the final player line against the correct date, opponent, and official/reliable game box before grading. The incorrect Bridges 25-point settlement imported his March 1 regular-season line against San Antonio into the June 3 NBA Finals game; the correct Finals Game 1 line was 9 points in 28 minutes.
- POSITIVE CONFIRMATION (both-unders cold-offense + availability-by-profile): the Mercury-Storm pregame card cashed clean 2-2 with the correct UNDER lean on both Q1 (36) and 1H (69) and the correct narrow winner (Phoenix 72-68). The new injury module worked on first full test: Seattle's gutted frontcourt (Magbegor/Malonga out) suppressed scoring as projected, and Phoenix keeping Thomas/Copper carried the narrow win. Repeat: net both teams' exclusions by profile and lean unders when both offenses are cold AND the depleted side loses interior scoring.

CORRECTED NUANCE: a tight historical H2H does NOT override current-form asymmetry when one side is surging and the other is depleted; cushion-discipline applies in near-even FORM matchups, not when the favourite is in dominant form against a weakened opponent. CORRECTED RULE (costly, repeated): weather and a stale favourable H2H compress margins between EVENLY-MATCHED teams, NOT in a quality/personnel mismatch. Essendon were in deep crisis (Solomon interim debut, McGrath/Redman/Durham out, five straight losses) and a healthy home West Coast simply controlled it. When the underdog is in genuine crisis (multiple core outs + coaching turmoil + bad record), rank the favourite cover HIGH even in ugly weather and even against a favourable H2H; the side axis must default toward the clearly superior/healthier team rather than the cushion. |

Top pick Under 152.5 lost, so add a top-pick penalty. Potential winner Astana lost. | Pregame context: Data cutoff 9:57pm AEST / 4:57pm Astana time, just before the 17:00 local tip. Official NBF Kazakhstan listed Game 5 of the playoff final at Sports hall Astana Academy, Astana, "Game not started". Series scores: Astana lost home Games 1-2 by 72-74 and 85-89, then won road Games 3-4 by 68-53 and 79-71. Finals totals: 146, 174, 121, 150. Official season comparison: Astana 33-8, +17.90 average margin, 86.51 PPG and 68.61 allowed; Kaspiy 23-17, +4.42 margin, 80.75 PPG and 76.33 allowed. Current series/H2H leaders from official comparison: Astana rely on Eric Day creation plus Robert Pan rebounding/effectiveness; Kaspiy rely on Eric McGill playmaking/steals and Vladimir Ivanov rebounding/interior efficiency. Grading plan: Top pick Under 152.5 gets a top-pick penalty if it loses; bottom pick Over 152.5 gets a bottom-pick penalty if it wins. Pregame analysis: This is a Game 5/title-decider profile where immediate series defence outranks season PPG. Under 152.5 ranks first because three of four finals games landed at 150 or lower, Astana have held Kaspiy to 53 and 71 since the series moved to must-respond mode, and Astana's season defence is far stronger than Kaspiy's. Astana -6.5 ranks second because they are the better full-season team, have home court, won the last two by 15 and 8, and have the efficiency/rebounding edge. Aktau +6.5 is live because they already won twice in Astana by 2 and 4, but it sits below Astana cover due to the momentum/defensive shift. Over 152.5 is bottom because it needs a return to the Game 2 174-point shot-making/foul/late-scoring environment rather than the tighter 146/121/150 finals pattern. Settlement retrospective: The card correctly identified the Game 5 decider context but still mis-ranked both axes. Two compounding errors: (1) leaned the title-decider total UNDER on a tight-finals pattern (146/121/150), but a winner-take-all Game 5 produced a 79-79 regulation shootout (158) and OT pushed it to 176 - in a decider both teams empty the tank and the high-scoring/foul/OT tail is much fatter than the prior-games average implies. (2) Ranked Astana's cover above the qualified underdog who had ALREADY won twice in Astana this series (by 2 and 4); a Game 5 between closely-matched finalists is a near coin-flip, not a -6.5 favourite spot. NEW DECIDER RULES: in winner-take-all/elimination games between closely-matched teams, (a) lean the total UP toward the series high, not the average, and respect OT risk; (b) treat the side as a near coin-flip and do NOT bury the underdog who already won games in the series; (c) never apply a regular-season margin/PPG profile to a Game 5. |

The cleanest path is a modest/controlled Q1 followed by enough second-quarter recovery to clear 84.5 by halftime, exactly like the May 12 H2H: 39 Q1, 86 half. 1H Over 84.5 ranks first because both teams' first-half averages combine to about 86.3 and Phoenix's defensive profile gives Minnesota enough scoring floor even without Collier/Juhasz/Cechova. Q1 Under 42.5 ranks second because Minnesota's league-best/near-best defensive profile and Phoenix's recent offensive wobble can hold the opener around 39-42. Settlement retrospective: PROCESS NOTE: the chat response for this card mistakenly presented BOTH halves as unders (1H Under top, Q1 Under second), which would have graded 2-4; the LOGGED card correctly led with 1H Over 84.5 and graded 2-2 clean. Grade of record is the log = 2-2. Mixed result lessons: the 1H Over read was right - the May 12 H2H (39 Q1 / 86 half) and ~86.3 combined 1H average correctly beat the season "elite defence" narrative, and Collier/Juhasz OUT did NOT suppress Minnesota (35 in Q1). The clear miss was Q1 Under 42.5: the opener exploded to 57, so the "controlled Q1" assumption failed - both teams started hot at home in a Commissioner's Cup opener. KEY FIXES: (1) keep the discipline that beat the under here - weight recent ACTUAL phase scores (H2H 39/86) over season pace narratives; (2) stop assuming a controlled Q1 when a home team plus cup energy can produce a track-meet opener; (3) ensure the chat-presented ranking MATCHES the logged ranking - do not invert direction between answer and log. |

Confirmed starters: NC Toda Natsuki, 2-5, 4.34 season and 0-1, 13.50 vs Samsung; Samsung Ariel Jurado, 3-1, 2.17 season and 0-0, 2.25 vs NC. Under 10.5 ranks second because Jurado can suppress NC and the weather has light wind blowing in rather than carrying out, so a 6-4/7-3 shape is plausible even at Daegu. Dinos +2.5 is live because it can cash on any NC upset and a Samsung one- or two-run win, including the 6-4 band, but the matchup gap keeps it below the top two. Over 10.5 is bottom: Daegu/Samsung bullpen volatility makes it live, but it needs NC to do enough against Jurado or the bullpens to turn a 9-10 run game into 11+. Settlement retrospective: Side read was right but the LINE was wrong on both axes. (1) Picked the favourite to cover -1.5, but a 6-0 H2H dominant team beating a weak opponent does NOT guarantee a 2+ run margin - Samsung won by exactly 1 in a tight 8-7 game; against KBO opponents even a clear favourite frequently wins by a single run, so prefer the moneyline/+run-line over laying -1.5. (2) Leaned Under 10.5 on the "Jurado suppresses NC + wind blowing in" theory, but 15 runs blew through it - Daegu is a hitter's park and Samsung's deep lineup (Koo 1.038, Choi .996 OPS) plus bullpen volatility produced a slugfest. NEW KBO RULES: (a) do not lay -1.5 on KBO favourites by default - one-run wins are extremely common, value the +2.5/underdog run-line and the straight winner instead; (b) at Daegu specifically, default the total UP unless both starters are elite and in-form - Jurado being good was not enough to overcome two strong lineups in a hitter's park. The card got the WINNER right (Samsung) - keep using H2H + lineup depth + home for the moneyline, just stop converting that into an aggressive run-line lay. |

Powerplay 51/2 after 10 overs. Pakistan were bowled out for 190, so Australia won by 41 runs and levelled the series 1-1. First-5-over exact figure not confirmable from public sources (powerplay was 51/2 at 10 overs). | Listed items 2-2, no penalties (picks 2 and 3 are exact opposites so exactly one wins regardless): Under 274.5 WON (231); first-5 over/under split one-each (5-over figure unconfirmed but card grade fixed at 2-2); Over 274.5 LOST (231). Top pick Under 274.5 WON (no top penalty); bottom pick Over 274.5 LOST (no bottom penalty). Potential winner Pakistan LOST. | Pregame context: At the live-start cutoff. Data cutoff 9:31pm AEST / 4:31pm Lahore, just after toss. Pakistan won the toss and elected to bowl, so Australia are confirmed batting first at Gaddafi Stadium, Lahore. CricTracker listed clear sky, balanced pitch, and average first-innings score 275. Indian Express described Gaddafi as stroke-maker friendly with true bounce/short boundaries compared with Rawalpindi. CricketWorld listed Gaddafi men's ODI average first innings 252 and noted Australia made only 200 in the 1st ODI after collapsing from a smooth start once spin entered. Series context: Pakistan lead 1-0. Australia remain transitional/understrength but have Short/Carey/Inglis/Green/Renshaw scoring paths; Pakistan have Shaheen/Naseem/Haris new-ball pace and Abrar/Arafat middle-over spin. Grading plan: Top pick Under 274.5 gets a top-pick penalty if it loses; bottom pick Over 274.5 gets a bottom-pick penalty if it wins. Pregame analysis: Split phase read. Under 274.5 ranks first because 275+ requires Australia to convert the better Lahore deck into a full 50-over platform after being bowled out for 200 in ODI 1 and showing middle-over spin vulnerability. First-5 Over 26.5 ranks second because the line is modest for Gaddafi's true bounce/short-boundary start and Australia were reportedly smooth early in ODI 1 before the sixth-over spin trigger. First-5 Under 26.5 is live through Shaheen/Naseem new-ball swing and a cautious response to the ODI 1 collapse, but it is slightly below the over. Full-innings Over 274.5 is bottom: possible on this venue, but it needs Australia to avoid the middle-over collapse that Pakistan's attack just forced. Settlement retrospective: Clean read on the headline total, wrong read on the winner. The transitional/understrength Australia narrative + ODI 1 collapse + Pakistan's spin (Abrar/Arafat) correctly beat the 275 venue average, and 231 validated ranking the total UNDER first. The side call failed because a modest total was defendable on the same slow surface: Nathan Ellis took four wickets and Australia's spin options helped bowl Pakistan out for 190. New rule: on a slow ODI pitch, do not assume the chasing side wins merely because the first-innings score is below a broad venue average; compare both teams' spin control, new-ball wicket threat, and ability to defend a 220-240 total. LESSON ON MARKET CONSTRUCTION: pairing two exact-opposite legs (first-5 Over vs Under) as picks 2 and 3 guarantees a 2-2 floor but adds no real edge and wastes two of the four slots. Also: could not verify the 5-over figure post-game because ESPNcricinfo blocks WebFetch (403) - for cricket phase markets, capture the milestone score live or use a fetchable source. |

Markets are two complementary pairs, so card grades 2-2 by construction; edge is the directional lean + ranking. Pregame analysis: Thesis: Portland's very weak defense (94 allowed, DRtg 14th) is the dominant scoring driver and drags the game above GSV's slow-pace/elite-D profile; blended game total ~170-174. 1H Over 82.5 ranks first (~58%): at ~50% of a ~172 total a half lands ~85-86, and Portland's defense yields points to GSV's efficient home offense from the jump. Q1 Under 41.5 ranks second (~53%): 41.5 is an aggressive Q1 line, Q1 is structurally the lowest/feeling-out quarter, and GSV's slow home pace most suppresses the opener before benches/fouls open it up (~40-41 Q1 expected) - coherent with a controlled Q1 then a higher Q2 pushing the half over. Q1 Over 41.5 is third (~47%): live if both run-and-gun starts hit, but fights the high line. 1H Under 82.5 is bottom (~42%): needs GSV's defense/pace to fully smother Portland for a half, against Portland's 180-total game profile. Carried-forward lesson applied: weight ACTUAL combined-scoring environment (Portland 180-total games) over a single-team slow-pace narrative. ADDED LEG (follow-up): Combined 2nd-Half Total Over 76.5 - leaned OVER (~63%); two teams averaging ~85-86 PPG project ~85 combined per half, so 76.5 is ~8-9 pts below expectation; only real fade is a blowout/garbage-time clock-kill, but a ~6-pt projected Valkyries win in a competitive Cup game keeps benches honest. Settlement retrospective: EXCELLENT card - best process result in the log. (1) Game-total estimate ~172 was EXACT (172). (2) The nuanced split read nailed it: "controlled/feeling-out Q1 then a Q2 surge pushes the half over" - Q1 landed 41 (under 41.5 by a half-point) and Q2 exploded to 51 (GSV 35) for a 92 half, exactly as described. (3) 2H Over 76.5 cushion logic held (80). (4) Winner correct. KEY VALIDATED METHOD (repeat this): compare the LINE to the researched combined-scoring ENVIRONMENT - here Portland's league-worst defense (94 allowed, DRtg 14th) created a ~172-total game, so the lines were LOW and the OVERS (1H, 2H) were right, while Q1 stayed under because Q1 is the structural feeling-out quarter and 41.5 was an aggressive opener line. The dual insight - total-environment for the half/2H overs AND Q1-structural-suppression for the Q1 under - is the template to keep using. Margin was underestimated (lean 90-84, actual 95-77 by 18) but side was right; minor note: a slow-pace elite-D home team can still win comfortably AND go over when the opponent's defense is the weak link. |

Grading plan: Top pick 1H Under 95.5 gets a top-pick penalty if it loses; bottom pick 1H Over 95.5 gets a bottom-pick penalty if it wins. Q1 Under 47.5 ranks second (~74%): Q1 is the structural feeling-out quarter, expected ~41-43 vs a high 47.5 line, but single-quarter variance (early three-point flurry) makes it slightly less safe than the half. Q1 Over 47.5 is third (~26%): live only if both contenders open fast. 1H Over 95.5 is bottom (~22%): needs a track-meet ~191+ pace between two grind defenses - the least-likely outcome. Carried lesson: do NOT blindly lean over in a high-scoring league - anchor to the matchup-specific H2H total and both teams' defensive quality. Live update: LIVE UPDATE (user flagged mid-game): Q1 ended Auckland 33-23 Canterbury = 56 combined. Q1 Over 47.5 has WON; Q1 Under 47.5 (ranked 2nd) LOST. 1H Under 95.5 (TOP pick) now needs Q2 <=39 and is the underdog (~28%); 1H Over 95.5 (bottom pick) now ~72% to win and would trigger a bottom-pick penalty. Card heading toward ~1-3 with a bottom-pick penalty - a near-inversion of my ranking. Critical error: CRITICAL ERROR - WRONG ANCHOR. I leaned UNDER on both legs by anchoring to a STALE multi-season H2H average (~171 from 18 games since 2019) plus the preview's "defensive grind" narrative, while IGNORING current 2026 form (Auckland 107, Canterbury 93 in their last outings) and the informational value of high lines (47.5/95.5 implied a fast expected pace). A 56-point Q1 exposed it. DIRECT CONTRADICTION of the same-day WNBA win: the "compare line to scoring ENVIRONMENT" method only works with CURRENT-SEASON inputs. NEW HARD RULES: (1) NEVER anchor a total to an H2H average spanning multiple seasons - use this season's per-game scoring and the last 2-3 games; (2) treat preview adjectives ("grind", "two-way", "defensive") as low-weight vs actual recent scores; (3) when offered lines sit well ABOVE a stale H2H, assume the market has current info I'm missing and re-research current pace BEFORE fading; (4) NZ NBL 2026 is high-scoring - default the baseline higher. Settlement retrospective: Confirms the critical-error lesson: anchoring a total to a stale multi-season H2H instead of current form produced a 2-4. Fixes already logged and promoted to a hard pre-commit check (current-season pace + last 2-3 scores FIRST; multi-season H2H is context-only; preview adjectives low-weight). This is the reference example for why the pre-commit checklist exists. Settlement addendum: the final winner lean was correct, but that does not rescue the phase-total model; grade phase markets by phase truth, then side/winner separately. |

Under 10.5 ranks second because 10.5 is a high bar at Suwon with Ko already through a clean first inning and both teams having enough starter/bullpen quality to land in a 5-4/6-4 band. (2) KT ML correct, winner correct, Over 9.5 correct. WHERE I WAS WRONG: buried Over 10.5 at #4 again -> bottom penalty (13 runs). My central estimate (~10) straddled the line, so per the complementary-pair rule the over should NOT have been auto-bottomed; the genuine least-likely should have filled #4. LESSONS: (a) do NOT finalize a side grade from a mid-9th live snapshot - late rallies flip run-line/ML outcomes (KT 7-4 became KT 7-6, turning LG +1.5 from a projected loss into a win); (b) the over-buried-at-bottom penalty recurs - keep applying the bottom-slot discipline that just saved the Philippines card; (c) official league source (eng.koreabaseball.com) resolved what 4 aggregator reads could not - go to the official box FIRST for KBO settlement. |

Pregame analysis: Applying the full-log audit (esp. bottom-slot discipline + current-form anchor). 1H Over 0.5 ranks first (~72%): a home favorite vs a weak, leaky Guam should score before the break most of the time, though Philippines' grindy 1-goal form keeps it short of a lock. Total Under 3.5 ranks second (~58-60%): Philippines' demonstrated current low-scoring profile (1,1,1 goals) points to a 2-0/3-0/2-1 type win under 4 goals, even allowing for weaker opposition. Total Over 3.5 ranks third (~40%): live because Guam ships goals to weak teams and Philippines is a clear step up, but it fights Philippines' low-scoring current form - COMPLEMENTARY-PAIR FLAG: my expected total (~2.5-3.5) sits right at the line, so the Under/Over total axis is a MODERATE lean (~60/40), not a confident fade. 1H Under 0.5 is bottom (~28%): a goalless first half is the genuine least-likely outcome (<30%) and earns slot #4 - it is the inverse of pick 1, well-separated from it (not a coinflip). Bottom-slot check passed: the buried over (3.5) is at #3 as a near-coinflip, and the true fade (1H Under 0.5) is at #4. Settlement retrospective: MIXED - right process, wrong total lean, but the NEW DISCIPLINE PAID OFF. WHERE I WAS RIGHT: top pick 1H Over 0.5 and the fade 1H Under 0.5 both correct; winner correct; and critically the bottom-slot fix WORKED ON ITS FIRST TEST - I flagged the straddling total pair as a ~60/40 coinflip and put the genuine fade (1H Under 0.5) at #4 instead of burying the over. The total went OVER (6), so had I followed the OLD habit of burying Over 3.5 at #4 it would have been ANOTHER bottom-pick penalty; instead the card graded a clean 2-2. WHERE I WAS WRONG: the total DIRECTION - I leaned Under 3.5 (~60%) but it was a comfortable over (6 goals, 3 in the first half alone). CAUSE: I under-applied opponent-adjusted scoring (improvement #2) - I weighted Philippines' raw 1-goal recent form (vs Laos/Vietnam/Indonesia, all MUCH stronger than Guam) without adjusting the expected scoring UP for a weak, leaky opponent. NEW/REINFORCED RULE: when a favorite's recent low scores came against clearly STRONGER opponents than today's, adjust expected goals UP - raw recent counts vs tougher foes understate the ceiling vs a minnow (same family as the Morocco mismatch-over lesson). Net: the ranking architecture saved the card even though the total read was wrong - exactly what the bottom-slot discipline is for. |

Over 7.5 ranks second because Washington's offense, Miami's recent 7-3/7-3 road scoring, Alvarez's smaller starter sample, warm dry weather, and Washington bullpen/defense create ordinary 8-10 run paths. Nationals +1.5 ranks third: home/elite offense make it very live, but the exact failure path (Miami by 2+) has already happened twice in this series and fits the current pitcher/bullpen edge. Under 9.0 is fourth because it needs the game to stay at 8 or below to cash; Meyer and Miami's bullpen make it possible, but 9 is only a push and 10+ is live. Settlement retrospective: SIDE right, TOTAL wrong. WHERE RIGHT: the log correctly made Marlins the winner/top side via the Meyer + bullpen + defense edge (Miami won by 3 through RUN PREVENTION - Meyer bounced back from his clunker and held Washington's strong offense to 1). Validates: a pitching-edge favorite can win by 2-3 even with a modest offense, so its +1.5 (and ML) is safe and the OPPONENT +1.5 is the real risk. WHERE WRONG: total direction - ranked Over 7.5 second and buried Under 9.0 at #4, but the game was a 5-run UNDER. I over-weighted the spot-starter/bullpen-game over-narrative and under-weighted Meyer's quality. CORRECTED RULE (now applied to Guardians@Yankees): ELITE/QUALITY STARTER -> lean UNDER; the "bullpen game produces runs" angle is WEAKER than "quality starter suppresses runs." Under 166.5 ranks second because Adelaide's elite defensive pressure, depleted attack, cool conditions, and the 128-point Round 3 meeting all support a controlled game. Over 166.5 is third and remains live through Geelong's 101.8-point scoring average or improved weather; it is not a confident fade. Crows +12.5 is fourth because it requires Adelaide's home defense and post-bye freshness to keep the margin within two goals despite the forward-line losses. Forced-complement note: the four options are two exact complementary pairs, so there is no genuine sub-30% bottom outcome; confidence in slot four is capped. SERIOUS settlement retrospective: TOTAL right, SIDE ranking inverted. The under thesis was strongly correct and even conservative: the game reached only 149, with 72 at halftime and 106 at three-quarter time. But that same low-total environment made a 12.5-point road-favorite cover harder and made the home underdog cushion safer. The model over-weighted Geelong's season scoring differential, historical H2H dominance, and Adelaide's forward absences while under-weighting Adelaide's elite defense, post-bye home resilience, and the side value of a compressed scoring game. Geelong's repeated direct-matchup inaccuracy was a missed warning: they kicked 9.14 in the Round 3 win and 10.14 here, while Adelaide's 11.9 conversion was enough to win. Hall-Kahan's late tackle and Curtin's late intercept also showed the replacement/debutant path was not merely a downgrade. Concrete rule change: in AFL low-total projections, never rank a double-digit away favorite cover above the home underdog cushion unless the favorite has a clear territory-plus-conversion edge; missing forwards may strengthen the under without strengthening the favorite's cover. |

Under 11.0 is fourth because 11 is only a push and 12+ has an ordinary path through Samsung's lineup, Koo's poor Samsung split, Daegu, or late bullpen fatigue. SERIOUS settlement retrospective: the favorite and total direction were both wrong at the confidence extremes. The model over-weighted Samsung's record, 7-1 season-series edge, Daegu's run environment, and the first two series totals while under-weighting Koo's immediate form and NC's ability to control contact. The live follow-up was an especially strong correction signal: NC led 4-3 with an 11-5 hit advantage by the bottom of the sixth, then finished with a 13-5 hit edge while Samsung went scoreless over the final four innings. The feared bullpen-fatigue surge never arrived; Koo earned the win and Chun closed it. The pregame note itself admitted that 6-4 or 5-4 remained live, which means Under 11.0 never belonged in the declared least-likely slot. Concrete rule change: on KBO overlapping-total cards, a broad high under must rank above a modest over when at least one credible starter has strong current form and the low/middle 8-10 run path is ordinary; season H2H and park reputation cannot bury that under, and a live hit/lead imbalance must override the pregame favorite narrative. |

Concrete rule change: in basketball finals involving two 90+ PPG offenses, do not make a full-game under the top pick or the over the bottom pick solely because direct H2H totals were low; require confirmed lineup suppression, pace suppression, or a phase trend that survives the latest playoff scoring form. The Rapid/main-game separation was essential and should be repeated whenever NZNBL pages show two same-team fixtures on the same date. The model correctly trusted fresh main-game phase evidence: recent Q1s of 39/40 and halves of 87/89 were not just under leans, they understated how slow this game became. The new calibration is not "NZNBL always under"; it is "if recent same-team main-game phase splits repeatedly sit under a high threshold, rank the phase unders first even in a high-scoring league." The full-game projection was too high by 52 points, so future NZNBL totals must allow a low-tail collapse when both teams' recent main-game starts are slow and neither side has a confirmed lineup/pace catalyst. |

The Round 3 meeting finished West Coast 92, Port Adelaide 90, total 182, but current recent totals lean lower: West Coast last five totals 140, 174, 159, 166 and 187; Port last five totals 150, 171, 146, 151 and 160. Settlement retrospective: good total and winner read, but the side-cover order should have been tighter. The under thesis was right by 21.5 points, and Port's no-cushion winner path was correct, but a 148-total game made each score too valuable for a -6.5 road cover to be materially safer than the home +6.5 cushion. The exact result landed on the half-point danger zone: Port by 6. Concrete rule confirmation: in AFL projected sub-170 games, especially when the favorite is away and the underdog has recent home resilience, keep the favorite cover and underdog cushion adjacent unless the favorite owns a clear territory-plus-conversion edge. |

Projection at card time: Swiss low-total control / 2-1 over-risk cluster; halftime 0-1 or 0-0. Probability table: Under 2.5 ~57, 1H Over 0.5 ~55, 1H Under 0.5 ~45, Over 2.5 ~43. #1 stress test: Under 2.5 loses if Switzerland score early and Qatar must chase through Afif/subs, or if Qatar defensive errors create a 3-0/2-1 path. #4 test: Over 2.5 needs three goals despite Qatar's striker/leader benching and Switzerland's defensive-control profile; not impossible, but it has more failure paths than a 0-0 halftime. LOW-EDGE FORCED RANKING: exact complements mean no true sub-30 bottom; do not overstate confidence. |
